# claude-packs: workflow bugs

Faults in how synths are built with this kit, logged before they are fixed, closed with cause, fix and what now catches it.

## Open

| # | Found | What happens | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| KWB-238 | 2026-11-25 CET, collider (`workorder check delay`: W3 refused for missing `trace-the-builders` and `read-the-dsp-program` with both on its line) | [closed, c6e3987c] reproductionOrderRefusals read a step's skills only from a bold `**Skills:**` line; the work order template, and every other check of `workorder check`, write a plain `Skills:` line, so in a reproduction every templated step that builds engine code was refused whatever it listed. Fix: the line matched with or without the bold. Caught by: test/unit/lib/reproduction-law.test.mts, the new plain-line case red before the fix (5 of 6) and green after (6 of 6). Also: this session's KWB-237 (just below) and the supernova2_monitor supervisor's KWB-237 were numbered at the same time; both stand. | lib/reproduction-law.mts reproductionOrderRefusals |
| KWB-238 | 2026-11-26 04:00 CET, the supervisor in supernova2_monitor (its WB-230; plan item 6) | [closed in the kit, this commit; measured by tools/refusal-ab.mjs once taken in] code-shape's "holds N statements" refused one statement as several when it held a brace that is part of an expression: every one of its 14 wrong refusals in supernova2_monitor (a braced initializer passed to a call, `push_back ({ ... })`, an object parameter with defaults, an object literal on the right of `=`). **Fix:** lib/code-shapes.mts `statementsOf` reads a `{` after `=`, `return`, `,`, `(`, `[`, `:` or `?`, or inside parentheses, as a value of its statement (paired with its `}` on a stack), except after `)`, `]`, `=>`, `>`, `mutable`, `noexcept`, `override`, `final`, `const`, `else`, `do` or `try`, where it opens a body; nothing else changes (a lambda in a call, a for with a body, three calls on a line count as before, compared with the installed copy). **Narrowed** the same day, after the replay (tools/refusal-ab.mjs, 9cbdade against b719877): read for every statement reader, it fixed code-shape's wrong refusals (12 to 1) and cost template-law four right ones (real copies of object literals and plant rows, merged into one statement and so too short to match); the reading is now an option, `statementsOf(text, lang, { valueBraces: true })`, taken by code-shape's per-line count alone, and the copy detectors read as before. **Caught by:** code-shape's selftest 25 of 25 (new: a braced initializer in a call and an object parameter one statement; three calls on a line still packed), the value-brace reading planted out and both new cases seen red; template-law 29 of 29 and once-only 23 of 23 (the same reader); their tests 73 of 73 with KF-27 (a `[&] {` lambda) and repo-lint. | packs/base/hooks/lib/code-shapes.mts `statementsOf` |
| KWB-237 | 2026-11-25 CET, collider (`workorder check delay` red on doctor alone) | [partly closed, this commit] doctor --strict judged two hooks over budget at load 10 to 13 with no canary in their events to mark them starved. (1) `look-gate`'s worst call 244 ms over 100 (its git children; its row from the KWB-228 just below left worst at the 200 default): worst 1000 like firmware-out-of-git, closed. (2) `reuse-hint` 83/484 ms p50/p95 over 6 prompts (every prompt finds its worker retired and reads the repetition index cold, as its row's comment says) [gap]: a first commit raised its row to 100/500/1000 and was pushed with hook-budget.test.mts red 2 of 25 (the tests pin the row at 80/200 as their worked example; my command chained the commit after the test without stopping on its failure); reverted here, 25 of 25. The fix is the daemon-side lookup the row's comment names, or a load-aware doctor (a lone slow call above the core count's load says the machine). Also: the KWB-228 just below was numbered while another session took KWB-228 to 236; both stand. | lib/hook-budget.mts HOOK_OVERRIDES; packs/base/hooks/reuse-hint |
| KWB-228 | 2026-11-25 CET, collider (`workorder check presets` red on doctor alone) | [closed, this commit] doctor --strict judged `look-gate` against the base hook budget (10/50 ms), and its 0/132 ms over 23 calls turned every `workorder check` red. Cause: look-gate is 0 ms on all but build and test commands, where it runs git children (rev-parse, diff, log, show) to see which looks a branch changed and whether each has its report: the same shape as git-guard and firmware-out-of-git, which have rows of their own; look-gate had none. Fix: its row in HOOK_OVERRIDES, p50 10 ms (the base) and p95 200 ms (the git children), with the measurement on its line; the way to remove the row is the same as theirs, an incremental look (the branch's changed paths cached by HEAD). Caught by: test/unit/lib/hook-budget.test.mts 25 of 25; collider's doctor --strict OK after the update. | lib/hook-budget.mts HOOK_OVERRIDES |

Worked through by `git fetch origin <branch>:refs/remotes/origin/<branch>` after each push. Measured: `definesOf` takes 24 ms a call on that file (0.2 ms on a Python file), and judgeWrite calls it twice. The java row's `define` regex in lib/languages.mts takes 0.3 ms on the raw text and 24.4 ms on the blanked text `definesOf` gives it: `(?:public\|...\|\s)+` followed by `[\w<>[\],\s]+\s` lets both parts take the same whitespace and newlines, so the long blank runs where comments were backtrack, and a match runs across lines. That is also a wrong answer: on the file it reports `run, for, for, while, for, if, if` as defined methods, six control keywords read as method names. **Fix:** `JAVA_DEFINE` in lib/languages.mts: modifiers, one type token (generics with one level of nesting, arrays), the name, parameters, an optional throws list and the brace, every separator before the name `[ \t]`, so a match stays on its line and no two parts take the same whitespace; a control keyword (`if`, `for`, `foreach`, `while`, `switch`, `catch`, `using`, `lock`, ...) is never the name or the type. On the file: 0.4 ms a call, names found `run`. **Caught by:** test/unit/hooks-lib/java-defines.test.mts, 3 cases (Java methods on their lines with no keyword or anonymous class; C# methods with none of foreach, using, lock; a 300-line file with long comment lines under 5 ms a call), 0 of 3 on the old pattern (commit 1837d869 planted it, reverted next: the 300-line file took 1,758 ms for its six calls), 3 of 3 with the fix; with languages, near-copies and template-law's tests, 22 of 22. | packs/base/hooks/lib/languages.mts, the java row's `define` |
